経済指標は景気回復の勢いがなお鈍いことを示す
内容。ユーロ圏鉱工業生産はおおむね横ばい圏の推

欧
州
経済指標は生産・輸出が底堅い一方、内需の弱さ
が鮮明。8月の鉱工業生産は伸びが加速。輸出も
高い伸びを維持。一方、小売売上高はほぼ横ばい、
投資は落ち込みが継続
中
国
消費者物価は政府支援策で伸び縮小も、物価上
振れリスクは残存。日銀は政策金利を引き上げ、植
田総裁は追加利上げ継続の方針を明言。輸出は

堅調も輸入の伸びが上回り貿易赤字は継続
日
本
先週・先々週（9/14～9/28）発表の主な経済指標
半導体や投資関連財の生産は堅調。AI投資の強さが実体面
にも波及していることを確認。企業部門はなお資金余剰を維持。
外部調達も株式中心で、債務調達の増勢は限定的
割高な米国産原油への代替調達で輸入額が増加する中、原
油タンカー運賃も急騰。中東産への調達回帰が進んでも輸入
負担は下がらず、貿易赤字を通じて円安圧力が残るリスク
注目点② 原油輸送費が急騰。貿易赤字長期化で円安圧力も注目点①旺盛なAI投資が生産を押し上げ。資金調達にも余力

半導体・関連デバイス
（前年比、％）
（年/月）
米国：生産は底堅く推移。AI・防衛が投資財生産をけん引、半導体増産も寄与
 2026年8月の鉱工業生産は前年比＋1.4％と底堅い結果
― 半導体関連の生産が引き続きけん引。生産能力が2022年対比1.7倍に拡大するなど生産体制の増強が背景。事実、主
要半導体メーカーによる工場新設・増強を受け、MOSロジック（GPU、CPUなど演算用）の量産が本格化
 AI・防衛分野を中心に投資財生産も堅調さを維持
― 投資関連財はAI・防衛が押し上げる形で同＋4.7％と増勢継続。国防・宇宙関連設備は同＋6.1％ と全体を上回る伸び。
地政学リスクの高まりを背景に軍用機やミサイル・防空システムなどの生産体制強化が図られている模様
4

（兆ドル）
資金余剰
米国：AI投資ブーム下で企業の外部資金調達は拡大も、債務調達の増勢は限定的
 非金融法人企業は資金余剰が継続
― 設備投資が高水準で推移する一方、企業部門全体では潤沢な内部資金が投資を下支え
 AI投資ブームのもと企業の外部資金調達は拡大
― 押し上げの主因は株式発行。債務調達は一定の規模を維持しているものの増勢は鈍化。投資を主導するハイパース
ケーラーの高いキャッシュ創出力に加え、株式による資金調達を反映した格好
5

### Page 7

米国：債務調達は全般に減速。企業金融で存在感高めるプライベートクレジットも拡大一服
 債務調達の内訳をみると、社債、銀行借入、プライベートクレジットとも前期から減速
― とりわけプライベートクレジットは2024～25年の拡大局面から2026年入り後は鈍化が鮮明。IT分野では成約件数が大
幅に減少、全体に占めるシェアも低下。ソフトウェア企業を巡る信用リスクへの警戒や案件組成の減速が影響した模様
 減速したとはいえ、プライベートクレジットは企業金融で一定の存在感
― 企業債務残高に占めるプライベートクレジットの割合は7％程度と銀行借入にほぼ匹敵。過去10年間では3倍に拡大
6

### Page 8

米国：AI投資の外部化でプライベートクレジットを含む案件型金融が拡大する可能性
 事業開発会社（BDC）による資金供給の縮小が際立つ。足元ではプライベート・デットファンドを通じた供給にもかげり
― プライベートクレジットの活用が限定的だった背景には、信用力の高いハイパースケーラーによるデータセンターやAI
サーバー投資が中心だったことも一因
 AI関連投資ブームのすそ野拡大に伴いプライベートクレジットなどの活用余地が高まる可能性
―SPV・JV、データセンター事業者、電力・送配電・冷却など周辺インフラへ投資主体が広がれば、個別案件のキャッシュ
フローや資産価値に応じた資金調達需要が高まることも。案件型資金調達の活用余地拡大には目配り
7
プライベートクレジットの供給主体別増減
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 9

日本：中東情勢悪化を受けて輸送コストが急騰。原油高と合わせて輸入負担を押し上げ
 原油輸入額の増加で貿易赤字が拡大
―6月以降、割高な米国産を中心に原油の代替調達が進む中で輸入金額が増加
 中東情勢悪化で原油タンカー運賃が夏場以降急騰
― 運賃は7月末から約3倍に上昇。今後、割高な米国産から中東産へと回帰する中でも輸入負担は下がりにくい可能性
― 貿易赤字が長期化し、円安が進めば、輸入物価を一段と押し上げるリスク
8

（年／月）
日本：CPIはエネルギー補助効果で小幅鈍化も、補助終了後は再加速のリスク
9
 8月の全国コアCPIは前年比＋1.7％（前月：同＋1.8％）と小幅に鈍化
― 電気・ガス料金への補助再開でエネルギー関連が下落。補助効果でCPIを0.2％Pt程度押し下げ
― 一方、既往の原油高に伴う最終財への価格転嫁が徐々に浸透するなか、ラップや洗剤等を中心にその他財価格が上昇
 こうした価格転嫁は今後も続くとみられるほか、電気・ガス料金の補助効果が剥落するもとで、エネルギー物価は再加速へ
― 原油高等が時間差を伴って電気・ガス料金に波及。補助効果が剥落する11月には、コアCPIを0.44％Pt程度押し上げへ

― また、原油価格の再上昇や輸送コスト上昇による輸入物価の上振れは、CPIの更なる押し上げ要因に
補助再開でエネルギー関連が下落
一方、原油高等の価格転嫁により、
その他財価格は上昇

日本：日銀追加利上げは最短インターバル。今後も日銀は3カ月に1回程度のペースで利上げへ
10
 日銀は9月会合で利上げを決定（反対2名）。利上げは6月以来3カ月ぶりで、今次局面では最も短いインターバル
―9月会合後の会見で植田総裁は、物価押し上げから物価上振れ抑制へと「政策の局面が変化した」と説明
― 政策金利は1.25％と、日銀による中立金利推計値のレンジ下限を上回るが、金融環境は利上げ後も緩和的と評価
 今後も日銀は3カ月に1回程度のペースで利上げを続ける可能性大
― 大幅利上げや連続利上げについては物価上振れリスクが大きい場合の選択肢と説明。急激な政策変更を回避する観点

から、先手を打って利上げを実施していく姿勢を強調
物価上振れリスクへの対応から
利上げ間隔が短期化
政策金利とブレークイーブン・インフレ
（注） ブレークイーブン・インフレは基調的物価上昇率の一面を捉えていると考えられるが、
必ずしも基調的物価上昇率そのものを示す指標ではない点に留意
（出所）日本銀行、LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成 （出所） 日本銀行より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
日銀9月会合のポイント（声明文・総裁会見）
声明文
• 企業の賃金・価格設定行動の積極化や中長期的な予想物価上昇
率の上昇などを踏まえると、2％物価目標を上振れるリスクがある
• 企業間取引における価格上昇圧力が消費者段階にも波及し始め
ているほか、賃金上昇の販売価格への転嫁の動きも続いている
• 政策金利の変更後も、緩和的な金融環境は維持される
会見での注目発言
• 基調的物価上昇率が2％に接近し、政策局面が変化。物価上振れ
を回避する観点が重要に
• 原油価格の上昇やそこから波及した物価上昇圧力、AI関連需要

や円安進行が幅広い品目の物価上昇圧力につながる可能性
• 基調的物価の2％定着を確認するのにはしばらく時間がかかる
• 金融緩和の度合いは利上げにより少しずつ縮まっているが、銀行
貸し出しや資産市場は依然として緩和的
• 急激な利上げで金融環境が大きく変化することや、それによる経
済へのマイナス影響を回避する観点は重要
• 大幅利上げに追い込まれる可能性を減らすように政策を運営する
注目点②原油輸送費が急騰。貿易赤字長期化で円安圧力も

2330 台積電(TSMC)

報告日期： 2026/10/16 研 究 員：羅 金 鳳 (Sunny Lo ) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要：  
⚫ 4Q26營收展望符合預期，但毛利率受 N2良率調整問題而未有
顯著改善，不過因 N2 的出貨量快速大幅成長，有利未來整體
毛利率的改善。  
⚫ 積極擴充產能， N3將擴充三個新廠，且 N2的產能在 2027年
將擴充一倍，而後段的 Interposer 則與 Global  Foundry 進行
合作。  
⚫ AI五年的 CAGR >55%，不含 Agentic AI 所驅動的 CPU強勁
需求，顯見 AI的需求遠優於預期。  
⚫ 我們將 2026/2027 年的稅後 EPS由 106.63/142.76 元，上修
至 111.36/152.74 元。

一. 4Q26營收展望符合預期，但毛利率仍受 N2影響  
3Q26 AI因 AI持續帶動先進製程需求強勁， N3營收比重由 2Q26
的 30%上升至 33%，N2營收比重也由 2Q26 的 3%上升至 8%，
帶動台積電 3Q26 營收達 14,942 億元， QoQ +17.62 %，
YoY +50.95 %，在成本改善、高產能利用率 及製程的升級帶動下，
抵銷 2nm量產初期對毛利率的負影響下， 3Q26毛利率 66.41%，
達公司展望的上緣， 稅後 EPS 2 9.43元，優於預期。 4Q26在 AI
帶動的先進製程及先進封裝需求持續強勁下，預估 以美元對新台
幣 1:31.8 為基礎計算，預估 營收中 值約 16,949 億元，
QoQ+13.4 3%，YoY+ 62.02 %，符合市場預期，惟 仍受 N2量產良
率調整問題的影響，預估 4Q26 整體毛利率維持在 66%左右，，
單季稅後 EPS 32.81元，續創新高 。

二. AI需求強勁，將擴大投資及合作  
由於客戶及客戶的客戶持續釋出 強勁的需求，AI 需求 5 年(2025
～2030)的 CAGR將超越 55%，且此一數字不含 Agentic AI 所帶
動的 CPU需求，主因 CPU主要的應用除了 AI之外，亦應用於傳
統的 PC及非 AI領域，顯見 AI需求的延續性拉長且需求優於預
期，台積電將積極擴產滿足客戶需求，雖然 2026年資本支出維持
600～640億美元，但因持續的海內外擴產加上 2030 年在成本合
理及正面效益下， 會開始導入 Hi-NA EUV，因此，預估未來數年
的資本支出將大幅走高。

     20 X 2027 EPS 
參考價位：2440元 
     15.97 X 2027EPS

2

AI帶動 GPU、ASIC需求持續成長，但進入 Agentic AI 則是帶動
CPU需求強勁成長，驅動半導體榮景延續。由於目前需求遠遠大
過台積電所能供應的產能，且持續朝先進製程應用，因此，台積電
目前 N2已在新竹、高雄量產，且 2027年產能將會擴增一倍，應
用於智慧型手機、 HPC、AI，且 A14 將會在 2027 年進行風險性
試產，2028年量產； N3方面也會擴增三個新廠，台南廠的進度符
合預期， 1H27可量產，亞利桑那州第二個 Fab(N3)在 3Q27也可
開始量產 (比預期提前 )，熊本廠於 2028年裝機量產。 由於台積電
美國大客戶及美國政府 希望台積電在美擴大產能，因此，台積電將
會在亞利桑那州以外的德州擴產，會由台積電自已營運 Fab廠，
不過目前尚未有正式結論。 雖然看似擴充相當大的產能，但台積電
都已由上而上的評估且有紀律的擴產，在電力等關鍵指標方面也
都已考慮進去了。

除了先進製程需求強勁外，先進封裝的需求也相對緊俏，因此，台
積電在後段將與 Global Foundry 合作，由 Global Foundry 進行
Interposer 的生產，以滿足客戶需求 ，台積電則是衝刺利潤率更好
的先進製程。

三. 上修 2026/2027年稅後 EPS至 111.36 /152.74元 
2026年受惠 AI及 Agentic AI 需求超乎  預期的帶動下，台積電將
2026年全年的營收由 YoY+40%上修至 44%(以美元計價 )，因此，
我們預估台積電 2026 年營收 55,936 億元，YoY+46.85%，毛利
率 66.55%，稅後淨利 28,877 億元，YoY+68.1%，稅後 EPS由
106.63 元上修至 111.36元。2027年在 GPU、ASIC、CPU、XPU
需求持續強勁的帶動下， 台積電營運仍會是強勁成長的一年，我們
預估台積電 2027 年營收 77,218 億元， YoY+37.17%，毛利率
67.53%，稅後 EPS由 142.76 元上修至 152.74 元。

四. 投資建議維持買進，目標價 3055元 
在 AI需求強勁的帶動下， 先進製程產能持續緊俏，台積電除了積
極擴擴 N3產能外， 2027 年 N2的產能將增加一倍 ，在高階產品
應用上將取得高市佔率， 且在毛利率表現方面，因 先進製程領導地
位的穩固吸引更多訂單， 訂價策略的改變， 及良率的改善，帶動毛
利率維持高檔，帶動獲利 持續大幅成長， 因此，投資建議維持買
進，目標價 3055元(20X2027年稅後 EPS)。

3 
一.公司簡介  
台積電(2330)成立於 1987年，為專業的 Fabless 半導體廠商，為
客戶生產的晶片廣泛地被運用在各種終端市場，例如行動裝置、高
效能運算、車用電子與物聯網等。 2019年，台積電及其子公司所
擁有及管理 約當年產能超過 1,200 萬片十二吋晶圓。在台灣設有
三座十二吋超大晶圓廠、四座八吋晶圓廠和一座六吋晶圓廠，並擁
有一家 100%持有之海外子公司 —台積電（南京）有限公司之十二
吋晶圓廠及二家 100%持有之海外子公司 —WaferTech 美國子公
司、台積電 (中國)有限公司之八吋晶圓廠。 台積電於 2011年第四
季開始導入 28nm，且開始加大先進製程技術的投資，於 2H12以
平面電晶體結構為基礎，在 20nm導入加強版的 HKMG 等技術，
並於 2014年進一步量產 16nm FinFET( 鰭式場效電晶體 )，持續推
進摩爾定律，儘管摩爾定律在 3nm以下將面臨物理極限，台積電
透過先進封裝製程延長摩爾定律，為高端產品 /客戶提供最先進的
技術。為因應美國製造優先及半導在在地化製造的需求，台積電於
2021年開始在美國亞利桑那州建廠，預計 興建六座先進製程廠、
2座先進封裝廠、 1個 R&D中心， 4nm已於 4Q24開始生產，預
估 3nm 也將在 2026 年進行量產，並且因為 AI 需求強勁的帶動
下，將加速 2nm及 16A的進廠速度，預估有機會於 2027～2028
年開始陸續量產。

二.營運概況及展望  
1、發展矽光子技術解決資料傳輸速度不足及訊耗損失問題  
10/2022 ChatGPT 推出後，其廣泛的推理能力，與使用者在各領
域以文字內容的方式對答如流，帶給使用者前所未有的體驗與 驚
豔，使用者呈現爆炸 性的成長，推升生成式 AI快速發展，使得 AI 
Server 呈現供不應求的現象 ，其不論在外觀的設計架構抑或 內部
的零組件規格甚至資料傳輸的頻寬及速度均較一般傳輸的 Server
大幅提升，且內部各元件的規格須同步提升才能使 AI Server 的強
大效能發揮最大的效用。

過往 Server 內部資料的傳輸採用銅線進行傳輸， 由於 AI Server
對資料傳輸的頻寬及傳輸速度的需求大幅提升， 銅線傳輸將產生
較大的訊號減損，使資料傳輸的品質不佳， 而以光做為資料傳輸的
解決方案，可解決資料傳輸訊號減損的問題， 因此，銅退光進的解
決方案勢在必行 。

矽光子的技術為銅退光進的最終解決方案， 但礙於技術及良率尚
不成熟，因此， CPO將為先行發展的技術，以 作為逐步達成光通
訊元件與主晶片直接在矽晶圓製程的階段即同時完成製作 的過渡
技術。在矽光子製作的過程中將以光波導取代 傳統銅線的傳輸，由

### Page 4

Company Report 
公司研究報告 
本報告僅供宏遠投顧內部及客戶參考，雖已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場
或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保
證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

4 
於光會沿著折射率較大的 物質前進，空氣的折射率為 1、水的折射
率為 1.33、玻璃的折射率為 1.46、二氧化矽的折射率為 1.6、矽
的折射率為 3.5，因此，光波導將選擇從矽晶圓上製作， 使光能進
行最佳的全反射，讓光能在光波導中的傳 送達最大的效率。由於矽
光子的發展為半導體未來的趨勢，大多數晶圓廠已投入研發，台積
電在此一趨勢下亦不缺席， 進行 40nm～90nm製程的矽光子技術
開發，預估 2025 年將利用 65nm 製程完成緊湊型通用光子引擎
(COUPE)的開發，支援可插拔式的連接器， 2026 年整合 CoWoS
封裝，將 CPO 與 Switch 晶片共同整合於同一基板上，完成共同
封裝光學 (CPO)的開發，未來將進一步將 CPO 與 XPU 共同整合
於矽仲介板上 ，實現晶片間更快的傳輸速度及較低的延時性。

圖一、矽光子發展示 意圖

資料來源： imec；宏遠投顧整理

2、透過 2.5D/3D 封裝延續摩爾定律  
矽為半導體重要材料，矽 1.0為平面式電晶 (Planar FET) 體時代，
由於電子遷移速度受到限制，製程節點 /閘極長度走到大約 28nm；
在 28nm以下進入到矽 2.0，為了改善電子元件漏電的問題， Intel
自 22nm 開始將平面世電晶體改成鰭式場效電晶體 (FinFET)，台
積電亦自 16nm開始將製程改為 FinFET，但當製程節點不斷往下
微縮至 5nm、3nm時，FinFET 將面臨摩爾定律的物理極限，即進
入矽 3.0時代，利用 2.5D及 3D封裝的技術可延續摩爾定律，台
積電在 2.5D封裝方面推出 CoWoS，Intel 推出 EMIB。CoWoS 在
晶片及基板之間加入 Si Interposer( 不具導電功能 )做為連接橋樑；
而 Intel 的 EMIB 則利用矽橋 (Silicon Bridge ；具有元件連通距離
短及反應速度快的特性 )做為多晶片之間連接的橋樑。

證。非經本公司同意，不得將本報告加以引用或轉載

圖二、2.5D封裝示意圖

資料來源： Intel & 宏遠投顧彙整

在 3D封裝方面，台積電推出 3D Fabric 平台，Intel 推出 Foveros 
3D封裝架構。台積電 3D Fabric，主要為前段製程採用 Wafer on 
Wafer 及 Chip on Wafer 的 SoIC(系統整合單晶片 )方式，將晶片
進行 3D堆疊，然後結合後段先進封裝製程，包括 CoWoS及InFO，
將邏輯晶片及記憶體晶片進行 2.5D及 3D的封裝製程，達到縮小
IC 面積及提高效能的效果。而 Intel 的 Foveros 將不同製程節點
的晶片進行 3D封裝，包括 10nm製程的運算晶片、 22nm製程的
系統 I/O 晶片和 PoP(Package -on-Package)封裝的 DRAM 記憶
體。

圖三、3D封裝示意圖

資料來源： 網路 & 宏遠投顧彙整

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

close on October 12, 2026.
New capacity announcements do not overturn our bullish view.
AI demand and increasing substrate complexity should keep the
market undersupplied from 2027, supporting further pricing and
margin expansion. We raise earnings estimates and price targets
for Unimicron and NYPCB and reiterate OW on both.
More capacity, but limited near-term relief: Suppliers are stepping up investment,

but several newly announced projects are not scheduled to ramp until late 2028-30
or beyond. Our updated supply-demand model continues to indicate undersupply
from 2027 through 2030, with tighter supply in CY27-28, which should only be
more favorable to pricing. Therefore, capacity additions announced in the past few
months do not, in our view, diminish the magnitude of pricing gains over the next
two years.
CY27 pricing should accelerate, amid tighter supply-demand in CY27-28, but

visibility on the magnitude remains limited: We see ABF substrate prices rising
25%+ in CY27, below buy-side expectations of ~50%. Our supply/demand outlook
points to undersupply next year, with larger substrates, higher layer counts, and
greater manufacturing complexity further constraining usable output. Raw material
cost pass-through was a key driver of CY26 price increases, we expect supply
tightness to play a greater role in CY27. This should allow pricing to outpace cost

inflation, driving faster margin expansion. We remain constructive on pricing, but the
magnitude and timing of increases depends on customer-level negotiations.
With pricing a segment-wide tailwind, we prefer Unimicron for its stronger CY27
capacity growth: As supply tightens, the ability to deliver incremental volume
should become a key differentiator. We favor suppliers bringing capacity online in
CY27 over those whose expansion plans contribute in later years. Unimicron’s

Guangfu II ramp should add 10-15% to capacity in CY27, compared with 5-10%
growth at NYPCB through debottlenecking, giving Unimicron greater scope to
benefit from both pricing and volume growth. Unimicron is also our preferred play
heading into earnings, as more demanding expectations for NYPCB leave less room
for an upside surprise. But we remain OW on NYPCB given its networking and AMD
CPU exposure, alongside its proactive pricing strategy.
We reiterate Overweight on Unimicron and NYPCB: Reflecting stronger earnings

trajectories, we raise our EPS estimates for both companies. Based on upside to our
revised price targets, our order of preference is Unimicron (NT$1,775 PT; 24x CY28e
P/E; 41% upside), followed by NYPCB (NT$2,000 PT; 22x CY28e P/E; 39% upside).
Potential risks: 1) Weaker demand for PCs, general servers, and AI servers; 2)
meaningful capacity expansion plans; 3) T-glass shortage; 4) CoWoP displacing ABF
substrates.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
October 14, 2026 08:00 AM GMT

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
Supply/Demand Update: Undersupply Deepens in CY27–28
Our updated model points to a wider supply shortfall in CY27–28, despite additional
capacity: Incorporating the latest expansion plans and semiconductor demand forecasts,
we now expect ABF substrate demand to exceed available supply by 9.3% in CY27 and
13.7% in CY28, versus 6.2% and 9.4% previously. This represents a widening of 3.1ppt and
4.3ppt, respectively, relative to our prior estimates. We maintain our view that CY26 is a

broadly balanced market, followed by increasingly tight supply over the next two years.
The main demand upgrade comes from other ASICs, including Chinese AI chips: We
raise our shipment assumptions for this category to 6.4mn units in CY27 and 8.7mn in
CY28, from 4.6mn and 6.2mn previously. Our Nvidia GPU and server CPU forecasts remain
largely unchanged, while we lower AMD GPU estimates while also keeping CY27 Trainium
and TPU shipments largely unchanged. The tighter substrate outlook therefore reflects

stronger demand across a broader set of ASIC suppliers, rather than higher shipment
assumptions for every major AI platform. We also raise our substrate content assumptions
for select chips based on our latest supply chain checks, lifting our substrate demand
estimates across some segments, including server CPUs and networking.
Higher supply assumptions provide limited relief in CY27-28: We now include additional
capacity announcements that have been announced recently, including two additional

Kinsus factories, scheduled to come online in CY28 and CY29, beyond the CY27 expansion
already in our model. Several larger projects, however, contribute later. NYPCB’s new site
starts ramping in 2H29, while SEMCO’s Sejong expansion begins mass production in
September 2028 and its project in Vietnam is scheduled to begin operations by June
2030. These additions increase medium-term supply but do not prevent the modeled
shortfall from worsening through CY28.

Increasing substrate complexity adds to the pressure on usable output: Larger body
sizes, higher layer counts, and more demanding manufacturing processes constrain the
output available to meet demand, even as suppliers expand installed capacity. With the
supply gap widening in CY27-28, we expect tightness to become a more important pricing
driver than raw material cost pass-through. This supports our forecast for 25%+ price
increases in CY27 and further increases in CY28, alongside continued margin expansion.

Supply-side developments
Unimicron (3037.TW, OW, covered by Howard Kao)
On September 30, Unimicron announced that it has acquired a Hukou site from
Bridgestone Taiwan for ~NT$10.089bn (~US$318mn). Based on our checks, this is for
capacity expansion plans in 2030 or beyond.
For CY26, Unimicron has announced capex of NT$53.7B.

Over the next few years, Unimicron already has several new production facilities coming
online, including:
•  Guangfu II: ramp in 2027
• Yangmei II: ramp in 2028
• Yangmei III: ramp in 2029
• Yangmei IV: ramp in 2029+
• Bridgestone: ramp in 2030+

### Page 4

M
Idea
4
NYPCB (8046.TW, OW, covered by Howard Kao)
On August 6, NYPCB announced that its Board of Directors had approved a capex budget
of NT$46.8bn (~US$1,474mn) to invest in building a new ABF substrate production site.
Our checks indicate that this production site is located in the southern part of Taiwan, and
will start to ramp up production in 2H29. Given the size of this investment, we estimate
that it will approximately double its capacity exiting CY30, relative to its CY28 capacity.

Kinsus (3189.TW, Not Covered)
In our previous model, we had assumed only one factory (k6b) coming online in CY27,
increasing capacity ~30%. However, recent checks suggest that Kinsus intends to bring
two more factories online, one in CY28 (k6c) and one in CY29 (k6d). This implies that
capacity would increase another ~25% in CY28 and ~20% in CY29, bringing Kinsus' total
ABF substrate capacity to almost double exiting CY29, relative to its CY26 capacity.

Ibiden (4062.T, covered by Shoji Sato)
Back in February, Ibiden first announced that it will invest capex of ¥500bn in the
electronics field planned through F3/29, for ABF substrate expansion. Sato-san's latest
view is that Ibiden will need a new factory building to install the equipment and facilities
that will be purchased with the ¥500bn. As a result, Sato-san expects Ibiden during its 2Q
results briefing (October 30) to announce plans to make a large capital investment,

including for a factory building with a long lead time, and raise its medium-term earnings
forecast (report here).
SEMCO (009150.KS, covered by Shawn Kim)
On September 29, SEMCO announced investment of KRW4.27 trillion (~US$3,178mn) in
Sejong and KRW2.51 trillion (~US$1,488mn) in Vietnam, for AI server semiconductor
substrate capacity expansion.
• Sejong: Investment period from September 2026 to May 2028, with mass
production starting in September 2028. Largest single-product investment to date,

serving as key hub for mass production of high-value-added, highly demanding
products, proactively responding to the increasing demand for AI server substrates.
• Vietnam: The Vietnamese subsidiary aims to complete construction and begin
operations by June 2030. It will be operated as a strategic hub to respond to global
volume demand and enhance production efficiency through a stable supply
system.
With this investment, Samsung Electro-Mechanics plans to actively respond to the

growing demand for high-value-added semiconductor substrates in high-growth markets
such as AI servers, HPC, and automotive electronics. In particular, this investment is being
pursued based on medium- to long-term cooperation with global big tech and
semiconductor customers. The investment includes customer funding support and
medium- to long-term volume guarantees; thus, the company expects to strengthen the
competitiveness of its FCBGA business based on a stable demand foundation.

LG Innotek (011070.KS, covered by Ryan Kim)

According to news reports (link), LG Innotek signed a memorandum of understanding
(MOU) with Haiphong, Vietnam, for an investment to expand its semiconductor substrate
plant. Under the agreement, LG Innotek will push to build a semiconductor substrate
plant in Haiphong.
•  Construction will begin in July 2026 and is scheduled to be completed in May
2027.
• The site spans approximately 330,000 square meters (98,000 pyeong), equivalent

to 45 soccer fields.
• According to a news report, LG Innotek will invest approximately $1 billion, or
about 1.53 trillion won, for its Phase 1 expansion plan, which mainly includes RF-SiP
(radio frequency system-in-package) and FC-CSP (flip chip-chip scale package).
• ABF substrates will be part of the Phase 2 expansion plan, but no details or
investment size has been disclosed, yet.
According to a press release from LG Innotek on September 30, LG Innotek started mass

production of ABF substrates for PC chipsets targeting big tech clients last year. It
initiated high-volume mass production of FC-BGA for PC CPUs in the third quarter of this
year. Simultaneously, the company is rapidly boosting revenue by securing additional
major clients. Starting next year, it plans to sequentially enter the AI accelerator and AI
server markets.
• Revenue from LG Innotek's package solution business has maintained an upward
trajectory for three consecutive years. With the package solution business

demonstrating tangible results alongside its optical solution business, the
company has strengthened its capacity to generate stable earnings.
• LG Innotek plans to rapidly expand its market share in the emerging semiconductor
substrate market, aiming to build its package solution business, including
semiconductor substrates, into a business generating US$743 million in operating
profit by 2031.
• In the future, LG Innotek plans to develop its future businesses, including

semiconductor substrates and physical AI solutions, to US$5.94 billion by 2030.
The company aims to have such future new businesses account for 25% of total
revenue, thereby completing a well-balanced business portfolio.
The expanded plant will produce major semiconductor substrates including radio
frequency system-in-package (RF-SiP), flip chip chip scale package (FC-CSP), and flip chip
ball grid array (FC-BGA). "The Gumi site will serve as a 'mother factory' responsible for

developing new semiconductor substrate technologies and producing new models and
high-value-added products, while the expanded plant in Vietnam will be operated as a
production base for general-purpose semiconductor substrates," an LG Innotek official
explained.

Daeduck (353200.KS, Not Covered)
Daeduck announced on July 20, 2026, that it is investing ₩497 billion (~US$370M)
manufacturing facilities and related infrastructure for semiconductor products, running

through December 31, 2027.
ABF capex is stepping up, but announced investment still falls short of implied
demand. We estimate that ABF substrate makers have announced ~US$14.3bn of
investment year to date, materially above the segment’s aggregate annual capex in recent
years, although these commitments span multiple years. Our tally is not exhaustive. LG
Innotek has yet to disclose the size of its planned ABF capacity investment, while Shinko
has not quantified the additional spending required to expand capacity following its

announced acquisition of a JDI fab.
Our supply chain checks suggest that each US$1 of investment can support US$0.50–
0.60 of annual revenue at full ramp. On this basis, the US$14.3bn announced so far could
support US$7.2–8.6bn of incremental annual revenue. This compares with US$11.4bn of
incremental revenue implied by our industry forecast. We model the ABF substrate
market reaching US$20.9bn in 2030, up from US$9.5bn in 2026. Despite the step-up in

capex announcements, the investments we can currently quantify appear insufficient to
meet our demand forecast, pointing to a need for further capacity expansion over the
coming years.

Morgan Stanley Research 13
The State of the Current Cycle
The ABF substrate market has gone through a few cycles over the past two
decades. We believe we are now still in the early innings of an up-cycle.
There was a prolonged down-cycle from 2012-18 as the PC TAM shrank amid the rise of
smartphones and tablets. During this tough period, companies were not able to generate
profits and had to compete on price to fill their idle capacity. As a result, during those

years, there was no or very limited capacity expansion, and capital expenditures by the
major ABF substrate suppliers were kept at a minimum.
Even as general server demand picked up, lifting utilization rates across the group going
into 2020, ABF substrate suppliers remained cautious on the longer-term demand outlook
and remained disciplined, hesitating to expand capacity. Hence, capex turned up just
slightly in 2019-20, increasing ~US$600mn vs. ~US$2.2bn on average per year in 2012-18.

This underinvestment led to sudden undersupply, as the shock from Covid-19 drove strong
work-from-home demand for PCs, as well as increased data usage and storage for general
computing driven by online entertainment. This ultimately led to a significant increase in
ABF substrate market value from 2020-22, reflected by the sudden surge in demand
along with meaningful pricing adjustments owing to the lack of supply.
During this 2020-22 period, much capacity expansion was set in motion as ABF substrate

suppliers and customers came to agree that necessary investments were needed for
future growth. Some subsidies were even given to these suppliers to facilitate this
capacity expansion. This is why capex jumped from ~US$2.9bn in 2020 to US$4.1bn in
2021 and US$5.5bn in 2022; even in 2023-24, capex remained elevated, as capacity
expansion plans continued.
Unfortunately, demand did not play out as expected. As the world came out of the
pandemic, demand for PCs and online entertainment fell sharply. This normalization of

demand, coupled with capacity expansion from practically every major ABF substrate
supplier, resulted in the ABF substrate market once again becoming oversupplied. This led
to a sharp decline in ABF substrate market value in 2023-24, as volumes normalized,
driving prices down to normalize against the new supply/demand dynamic.
Fortunately, cloud demand remained strong for general computing, and with AI demand
starting to pick up, the down-cycle this time around was much shorter (two years) vs. the

prior down-cycle (six years).
In 1H24, the ABF substrate market troughed and we saw an upward inflection since then,
but the pace of the recovery has been moderate and bifurcated. Midrange/low-end
markets such as PCs have shown moderate demand recovery, with high-end server and AI-
related end-demand recovering and growing at a faster pace. This is why we turned the
page on our bearish stance in 1H25, but did not turn more bullish until early 2026.

### Page 14

M
Idea
14
Periods Description
Period I (2010-2012): PC-centric
equilibrium
ABF substrate demand is primarily driven and dominated by CPU and GPU demand for desktops, notebooks,
discrete GPUs, with a meaningfully smaller exposure towards server CPUs.
Period II (2012-16): PC decline
and demand dilution
Global PC demand entered  a structural down-cycle as consumer behavior changed, with smartphones and tablets
absorbing more consumer time spent. This resulted in lower volumes and increased pricing competition as

suppliers fought to fill idle capacity.
Period III (2016-18): The quiet
inflection - servers and early
HPC
During these years, the PC market matured, while cloud computing started to take off. At the same time, server
CPU substrates quietly grew larger with more layers. We also started to see early accelerators being adopted in
high performance computing.
Period IV (2018-2022): WFH
demand driven structural
shortage
The Covid-19 pandemic drove massive work from home demand for consumer electronics and cloud computing.

This sudden surge in PC demand and increased time spent on gaming and online entertainment, bundled with a
continuous increase in substrate package size and layer count, resulted in a structural shortage, which also resulted
in longer lead times and raised prices. It was during this period (2021-22) that ABF suppliers globally started to
invest in new capacity.
Period V (2022-2024): Post-
pandemic correction and new
capacity arrival
As the world came out of the Covid-19 pandemic, WFH demand faded and  PC and cloud computing demand were

negatively affected. But at the same time, the new investments and capacity additions were just starting to slowly
come online, resulting in a drop in utilization rates and pricing.
Period VI (2024-2026):
Bifurcated recovery
PC demand normalized at the pre-Covid levels, with some replacement demand driven by Windows upgrade, while
the recovery and growth of AI and general server demand for cloud picks up at a meaningfully higher growth
trajectory.

Period VII (2026-30): AI diffusion Demand for AI proliferates from training to inference, driving ABF substrate market growth to re-accelerate over the
next five years after a two-year down-cycle following the Covid-19 pandemic. This drives increased demand for AI
computing, networking, and cloud computing, while demand for PCs remains soft.

Breaking down ABF substrate end demand
The end market mix for ABF substrates has shifted dramatically over the past decade. PCs

were once the dominant demand driver, accounting for approximately 70% of global ABF
substrate market value in 2015, supported primarily by PC CPUs and GPUs. Since then,
however, the industry's demand profile has undergone a structural transformation. We
estimate that PC-related applications represented only ~23% of total ABF substrate
market value in 2025, and this share is expected to decline further to below 10% by 2030.
In contrast, demand from servers, AI GPUs, AI ASICs, and networking applications has

expanded rapidly. We estimate that these segments collectively accounted for only
around 10% of the ABF substrate market in 2015, but their share increased to ~60% by
2025. Looking ahead, we expect they will represent more than 80% of total ABF
substrate market value by 2030.
In our view, this structural shift in end-market demand has been the primary driver of ABF
substrate industry growth over the past decade and will become even more pronounced

over the next five years. As AI infrastructure deployment accelerates, we expect continued
expansion in server, AI accelerator, and networking demand to drive meaningful
acceleration in ABF substrate market growth.

Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our 12-month price target increases ~21% to NT$1,775 (from NT$1,465), driven by our
increased earnings estimates for CY26-28: We believe the company's earnings have
returned to a growth track from 2026 onward and will show stronger  growth in 2027,
driven by the high-end ABF substrate market's reversion to undersupply and Unimicron's
dominant supply share in AI ASICs and also server CPUs, which puts it in a favorable

position to benefit from rising AI investments.
We believe the overall ABF substrate market bottomed in 2H24, and see growth
accelerating from 2H26 onward with the ramp of multiple next-generation, large body size
ABF substrates for new server GPUs, CPUs, ASICs, and networking chips. New
technologies such as embedded passives and Intel's EMIB-T should also increase the
manufacturing complexity of the package substrates, which adds to the under-capacity as

usable output decreases.
Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware companies within our coverage, we use a
residual income (RI) valuation model to value Unimicron – we think it derives the most
accurate value of the firm as it takes into account cost of equity. Our key assumptions
include:
• Cost of equity of 9.2% (unchanged)
° Risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield);
° Equity risk premium of 8.7%;
° Beta of 1.0;
• Medium-term growth rate of 14% (slightly lowered from 15%)

• Terminal growth rate of 3% (unchanged, similar to the rest of the companies under
our tech hardware coverage).
Our bull and bear case values increase by similar magnitudes, to NT$3,030 and NT
$595, respectively.

Price Target and Valuation Methodology
Our price target increases ~29%, to NT$2,000, reflecting our raised earnings
estimates for CY26-28: We believe the company's earnings have returned to a growth
track from 2026 onward and will show stronger growth in 2027, driven by the high-end
ABF substrate market's reversion to undersupply, NYPCB's dominant supply share for
networking chips, and its proactive pricing strategy, which puts it in a favorable position to

benefit from rising AI investments.
We believe the overall ABF substrate market bottomed in 2H24, and growth should
accelerate from 2H26 onward with the ramp of multiple next-generation, large body size
ABF substrates for new server GPUs, CPUs, ASICs and networking chips. New
technologies, such as embedded passives and Intel's EMIB-T, should also increase
manufacturing complexity of the package substrates, adding to the overall undercapacity
as usable output decreases.

Similar to our analysis of other tech hardware companies within our coverage, we use a
residual income (RI) valuation model to value NYPCB – we think it derives the most
accurate value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. Key assumptions
(all unchanged):
• Cost of equity of 9.3%;
° Risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield);
° Equity risk premium of 8.7%;
° Beta of 1.0;
• Medium-term growth rate of 17%  –  fair given our analysis of the substrate market

over the next couple of years;
• Terminal growth rate of 3% (similar to the rest of the companies under our tech
hardware coverage).
Our bull and bear case values increase by similar magnitudes, to NT$3,575 and NT
$1,020, respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Greater China Technology Hardware  | Asia Pacific
Monthly Databook: Sep-26
Shipments -2% vs. MSe;
Lowering 4Qe by 3%
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+
Howard Kao
Equity Analyst
Howard.Kao@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2989
Irene Yen
Equity Analyst
Irene.Yen@morganstanley.com                                                +886 2 2730-2869
Morgan Stanley Asia Limited+
Andy Meng, CFA
Equity Analyst

Andy.Meng@morganstanley.com                                                +852 2239-7689
Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
Overall September ODM NB shipments came in 2% below MSe: Quarter-end pull-
ins drove notebook shipments up 32% m/m, although volumes were 2% below MSe,
with weakness mainly coming from Wistron and Compal, and to a lesser extent,
Pegatron, reflecting slower NB shipments from Dell, as Dell accounted for ~60% of

Compal's NB shipment, and ~55% of Wistron's in CY25, on our estimates. Thus total
3Q NB builds came in at 25.2M units (-17% q/q, -26% y/y). This sequential decline
is sub-seasonal vs. the 15Y average seasonality of +5% q/q in 3Q.
Pull-forward demand appears to have come to an end, channel partners and
distributors have stocked up on inventory, and OEMs are slowing their sales into the
channel. We believe this is why our conversations with the ODMs suggest a slightly

weaker 4Q volume outlook than previously expected, despite favorable mix shift.
Thus, we lower our 4Q NB build estimate by 3% to 23.1M (-8% q/q, -29% y/y).
This sequential decline is sub-seasonal (despite an already lowered base in 3Q) vs.
the 15Y average seasonality of flat q/q in 4Q. We forecast October NB builds at
6.8M (-30% m/m, -33% y/y). Our revised forecasts imply CY26 builds of ~107.8M
units (-16% y/y) for the top five Taiwan NB ODMs.

Key Takeaways
Top five ODMs' September 2026 NB shipments were 9.7M units (+32% m/m, -
20% y/y), 2% below our estimate.
Total 3Q26 NB builds came in at 25.2M (-17% q/q, -26% y/y).
We see October 2026 NB builds at 6.8M units (-30% m/m, -33% y/y).
We lower our 4Q26 NB build estimate by 3% to 23.1M units (-8% q/q, -29% y/y).
Our top five ODMs' CY26 NB build estimate is ~107.8M units (-16% y/y).

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

6274 台燿 
(Taiwan Union Technology Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/08 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收符合預期， 毛利率受 1H26 漲價提升 ， 預期 3Q26 毛利
將順利反應成本上漲與優化之產品組合 ， 毛利率預估提升 3%+ 。  
 AI 伺服器帶動高階銅箔基板需求強勁，同時驅動高階網路交換
器升級動能，銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸
續採用 CCL M8、M9 規格。 
 2H26 低軌 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火箭搭載低軌衛星，所採用之銅
箔基板升級至 M8 等級；以及，1.6T 光模組需求量增帶動銅箔
基板需求量增。

一. 2Q26 營收與財報符合市場預期，3Q26 營收優於預期  
台燿 2Q26 營收為 143.01 億元，+42.2%QoQ、+110.93%YoY，毛利
率為 29.72% ，稅後淨利為 23.42 億元，單季稅後 EPS+7.82 元。2Q26
台燿受到 ASIC CCL 專案出貨帶動，產品組合優化，毛利率已相較
1Q26 季增 4.5% 。台燿已公告 3Q26 營收為 179.48 億元(優於市場預
期 3%)，+25.50%QoQ，毛利率可順利轉嫁且高階 CCL 出貨比重提
升，預估台燿 3Q26 毛利率為 33.21%，稅後淨利 36.72 億元，單季
稅後 EPS+12.60 元。

二. AI 伺服器帶動 CCL/PCB 產值提升，產能擴充 33% 
宏遠投顧觀察，AI 伺服器與先進封裝等需求日增，高階 CCL 市場
需求將在 2024 年~2026 年 CAGR 達 26% ， 而高階 CCL 供給端 CAGR
則是 7%，高階市場需求殷切。高階銅箔基板(包含 AI 伺服器和高
階網路交換器，自 2024 年始採用 M8 CCL 材料；2026 年 M8 材料
佔總體 CCL 市場 40%，M8 材料佔比顯著高於 2022 年、2023 年的
22%和 26%。台系銅箔基板供應鏈當中，M6~M8 等高階材料的供
應廠商為(2383)台光電為首、(6274)台燿次之。回顧 3 年來，台燿
產能擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開
出，台燿總產能為 260 萬張/月，+13%YoY。展望 2027 年，台燿泰
國廠、常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120
萬張/月，預估 2027 年底時，總產能達 380 萬張/月，+46%YoY；預
估 2028 年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平
均 3 年每年產能擴充約 33% 。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交
投資建議：買進

換器(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿
市佔率得以提升 。 2027 年隨著 NVIDIA Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+ 、
M9 材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，

年起 ， 除一般型伺服器 Intel Diamond Rapids 平台 、 AMD Zen6 Venice

數 46~52L、玻纖 Low Dk2/Q 布、銅箔 HVLP4/5) ，預期 CCL 廠商產
品規格顯著升級。

三.  台燿高階網路 Switch 板材、低軌衛星板材、1.6T 光
模塊板材，27 年規格朝向高階型號發展 
展望 2H26，在高階 Switch 升級方面，800G Switch 採用 M8、M9 
CCL 規格，2H26 台燿將新增高階 Switch 客戶，隨著 AI 伺服器、
高階網路交換器產業發展朝向 800G、1.6T Switch 升級趨勢帶動之
下， 
銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸續採用 CCL M8、
M9 規格。此外，2H26 開始 800G 與 1.6T 光纖收發模組(採用 M8、
M9 CCL)開始放量，AI 資料中心帶動光纖收發模組需求，400G 以
上光纖收發模組出貨量 2026 年 1 億個、2027 年 1.45 億個、2028 年
達到 1.6~1.65 億個；在中系系統廠商積極拉貨之下，1.6T 光纖收
發模組 2026/2027/2028 年估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M ， 1.6T 光纖
收發模組，於 2 年內呈倍數增長。低軌大型 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火
箭搭載低軌衛星 ， 所採用之銅箔基板升級至 M8 等級 ， 材料損耗要
求降低，訊號傳出完整性標準提高，對於 CCL、PCB(HDI)、玻纖
與銅箔等規格預期同步升級，推升材料成本與 PCB 產值。

四.預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 38.49/66.22 元 
2026 年受惠 AI 伺服器及 ASIC 需求持續強勁的帶動下，台燿為網
通、高階網路交換器銅箔基板重要供貨廠商，將受惠 AI Server 對
於高階銅箔基板強勁的需求，因而，預估台燿 2026 年營收 618.78
億元，+108.01%YoY，毛利率 31.13%，稅後淨利 114.79 億元，稅後
EPS+38.49元 。 隨泰國廠產能釋出 ， 預估台燿 2027年營收增至 963.61
億元，+55.73%YoY，毛利率為 33.26%，稅後淨利 198.23 億元，稅
後 EPS+66.22 元。

動，一般型伺服器 CPU 廠商 Intel 、AMD 均有新品帶動新平台升級
與相關供應的零組件商機，以及攻入 AI 伺服器、AI 高階網路交換

貳、公 司 概 況

一. 公司簡介 
台燿(6274)為銅箔基板廠商，主要產製雙面印刷電路板與多層印刷
電路板之銅箔基板 、 內層銅箔基板 、 黏合片 ， 產品應用別當中 ， ELL
與 SLL(M7、M8)佔比 39%、VLL 與 LL(M4、M6)佔 31%、HDI 與 N-
LL 佔 16%、其他品項佔 14%。

二.2026~2027 年營運概況及展望 
1.HDI 與 M8 以上升級仍為關注焦點，2026~2028 年產能建置積極 
過往一般型伺服器用板採用 HDI 設計，強調細線路設計，CPU 尺
寸變大 Pin Count 數量增加至 2,000 只~4,000 只 Pin Count，PCB 板
面積不變之下要走向細線路化(線寬/線距)朝向更細線路設計。製
程上 HDI 經過多次壓合後，PP 在熱壓多次後，每一層熱膨脹係數
不同，導致通孔 Crack 或翹曲，製作時容易產生爆板，因此對於
PCB 板廠製作良率會是一大技術難關，而金像電、健鼎等廠商於
高階 HDI 相對具備生產優勢與競爭力，相對應台光電、台燿等高
階 CCL 供貨與升級也成為製程一大瓶頸；回顧 3 年來，台燿產能
擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開出，
台 燿 總 產 能 為 260 萬 張 /月，+13%YoY。展 望 2027 年，台 燿 泰 國 廠、
常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120 萬張/
月 ， 預估 2027 年底時 ， 總產能達 380 萬張/月 ， +46%YoY ； 預估 2028
年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平均 3 年
每年產能擴充約 33%。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交換器
(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿市佔
率得以提升。2027 年隨著 Nvidia Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+、M9
材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，台
光電與台燿等高階 CCL 新產能釋出，產品組合持續優化。

針對 AI GPU 與 ASIC 發展進程，Google TPU 出貨量從 2026 年 400
萬顆上修至 2027 年(V8 增量)增至 800 萬顆，倍數增長。AWS ASIC 
預計 2027 年出貨量達 330~350 萬顆，增長+21%YoY 。NVIDIA 體系
GPU，Rubin 系列層數提升至 24~26L，材料端從 M8/M8+往更高規
格升級，更高層數與多層式架構，促使 PCB 板訊號走線、供電完
整性、高速訊號之阻抗匹配控制、訊號傳輸完整性，皆帶動 PCB

二. AI 伺服器拉貨、高階網路交換器、低軌衛星材料、

器 CPU 廠商 Intel 、 AMD 均有新品帶動新平台升級與相關供應的零

衛星 CCL 與新增的 1.6T 光纖收發模組規格轉換成長可期 ， 且台燿
在高階的網通 、 網路交換器板材供應 ， 具有一定之成本優勢與產品
結構相對穩健。預估台燿 2026 年營收 618.78 億元，+108.01%YoY ，
毛利率 31.13%，稅後淨利 114.79 億元，稅後 EPS+38.49 元。隨泰
國廠產能釋出，預估台燿 2 0 2 7 年營收增至 963.61 億 元 ，
+55.73%YoY，毛利率為 33.26%，稅後淨利 198.23 億元，稅後
EPS+66.22 元。

证券研究报告 
宏观经济点评 2026 年 10 月 17 日

9 月经济数据点评：生产偏强、需求渐进修复

[Table_Author] 
 魏争 分析师  沈夏宜 分析师

Email:weizheng@lczq.com Email:shenxiayi@lczq.com  
证书:S1320524100001 证书:S1320523020004

### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
6.2%升至 9.8%，我们理解部分原因是地缘局势提高能源安全和保供要
求，国内油气增储上产持续推进，为采矿业生产提供了支撑 。二是电热燃
水业增速回升。电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增加值增长 5.6%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点。从实物产量看，核电、风电和太阳能发电分别
增长 21.5%、10.6%和 19.5%。 
服务业生产明显改善，生产性服务业延续支撑，生活性服务有修复。9
月，服务业生产指数同比增长 4.8%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点；一是数
字信息和生产配套型服务业继续保持较快增长。 信息传输软件和信息技术
服务业、租赁和商务服务业、交通运输仓储和邮政业生产指数 分别增长
9.8%、9.5%和 5.4%。二是服务业改善广度有提升。住宿和餐饮业生产指
数增长 5.3%，服务业商务活动指数由 49.3%升至 50.2%，重新进入扩张
区间；电信广播电视及卫星传输、货币金融和保险等行业商务活动指数均
位于 55%以上1。 
投资低位企稳。9 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 7.2%，降幅与 8 月持
平。按累计金额反推，9 月当月投资同比约下降 7.2%，较 8 月的下降
11.0%明显收窄；剔除房地产后，当月投资同比约下降 3.4%，降幅较 8
月收窄约 4.4 个百分点。季调环比增长 0.07%，是 3 月以来首次转正。 
制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域 。9 月，制造业累计投
资下降 2.1%，降幅较 1—8 月收窄 0.2 个百分点。结构上看，一是正增行
业仍主要集中在装备和新兴制造领域。国家统计局公布的 13 个制造业行
业中，只有 5 个保持正增长，其中铁路船舶航空航天、计算机通信电子设
备制造业投资分别增长 15.8%和 9.6%，纺织、电气机械和通用设备制造
业分别增长 4.2%、1.2%和 1.1%。高技术产业投资增长 4.9%，其中信息
服务、航空航天器及设备、电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长 20.2%、
12.5%和 7.6%。二是上游原材料和下游消费制造业投资仍较弱。农副食
品加工、食品制造、化工、医药、有色金属冶炼、金属制品、专用设备和
汽车制造业投资分别下降 2.5%、10.3%、7.5%、8.1%、5.1%、10.1%、
8.9%和 6.3%。 
地产继续承压，价格端修复难抵销售走弱。9 月，地产投资累计同比下降
20.6%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.7 个百分点；按累计金额反推，9 月当月投资
下降约 26.3%，降幅较 8 月扩大约 0.8 个百分点。结构上看，一是地产销
售再次走弱，约束投资预期。 商品房销售面积和销售额累计分别下降
13.2%和 13.9%，降幅较 1—8 月扩大 1.1 和 0.9 个百分点；按累计金额反
推，9 月当月销售面积和销售额分别下降约 20.7%和 20.1%，较 8 月的下
降 14.8%和 12.1%明显走弱。东部地区销售额降幅由 12.6%扩大至
13.4%。30 大中城市商品房成交面积同比下降 10.9%，较 8 月扩大约 7.5
个百分点，其中一线城市增速由 18.8%降至 2.4%，二线城市降幅由
15.8%扩大至 22.7%，三线城市降幅基本持平。二是供给和资金端仍然偏
弱。新开工面积累计下降 24.9%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.1 个百分点，竣工面
积降幅由 23.7%扩大至 25.1%。房企到位资金下降 21.6%，降幅扩大 0.6

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
个百分点，其中，自筹资金降幅由 20.0%扩大至 21.4%；国内贷款和个人
按揭贷款降幅则分别小幅收窄至 33.1%和 22.1%。三是价格端有积极变
化。9 月，70 城新房和二手房价格同比分别下降 3.0%和 4.8%，降幅均较
8 月收窄 0.3 个百分点；环比看，新房降幅由 0.2%收窄至 0.1%，二手房
下降 0.3%，与上月持平。一线城市新房和二手房价格分别上涨 0.1%和
0.2%，二线城市新房价格由下降转为持平、二手房降幅收窄。 
基建投资继续放缓，项目端景气 有改善迹象。9 月，基建投资累计同比下
降 4.2%，降幅较 8 月扩大 0.2 个百分点。一方面，部分传统基建领域仍
然偏弱。交通运输仓储和邮政业投资增长 1.9%，较前期回落 0.2 个百分
点；水利环境和公共设施管理业投资下降 12.8%，降幅扩大 1.8 个百分
点，其中水利管理和公共设施管理业均下降 12%以上。电热燃水投资下降
6.8%，降幅较前期小幅收窄。另一方面，项目储备和部分结构性领域出现
积极信号，但向施工和实物工作量的传导仍不充分。9 月，建筑业商务活
动指数由 46.9%升至 50.3%，从业人员和业务活动预期指数也有所提高。
信息传输、航空运输和水上运输投资分别增长 30.8%、13.8%和 13.5%，
交通、能源、数据等“六张网”相关领域仍有支撑。 建筑业新订单指数由
42.4%升至 45.7%，尚未回到扩张区间，指向前端项目需求有改善，但向
施工和实物工作量的传导仍不充分。 
消费是内需端改善最明显的分项，商品消费仍受汽车拖累 。9 月，社零同
比增长 1.1%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.35%，剔除汽车后
社零同比增长 3.9%，较 8 月加快 1.4 个百分点。结构上看，商品和餐饮
消费同步恢复。9 月，商品零售额增长 1.0%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点；
餐饮收入增长 2.1%，提高 1.0 个百分点。从更广义的消费口径看，服务
消费仍是消费的重要支撑。三季度，社会消费商品和服务零售总额增长
2.6%，其中服务零售额增长 5.0%，明显高于商品零售额的 1.0%；网上
服务零售额增长 5.3%。通讯信息、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务零售额
保持较快增长。商品消费方面，必选消费和通讯器材维持韧性，汽车是主
要拖累。服装、化妆品、日用品、家电和中西药品零售额分别增长
1.2%、5.4%、4.1%、1.9%和 5.0%，通讯器材类增长 31.1%。汽车零售
额下降 21.2%，降幅较 8 月扩大 2.7 个百分点；家具、建筑装潢材料仍分
别下降 3.0%和 9.2%，降幅较 8 月有所收窄。 
展望后续，四季度经济有望延续生产较强、需求渐进修复的格局，未来增
量支撑主要来自两条线索。总体看，三季度实际增长边际企稳，优势制造
业、外需和服务消费构成主要支撑。往后看，增量支撑线索有两方面。一
方面，以“六张网”和 8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具为抓手，加快资金
投放和项目开工。9 月以来三家政策性银行投放的新型政策性金融工具资
金已经超过 1000 亿元2，建筑业 PMI 和新订单指数同步回升，指向政策
正在向项目端传导。不过，从补充资本金、落实配套融资到形成施工和实
物工作量仍存在时滞，基建对传统建筑链的拉动或更多在四季度后半段逐
步显现。另一方面，服务消费仍是扩大内需更具确定性的方向。9 月多部
门出台金融支持服务业扩能提质政策，叠加扩大消费“十五五”规划对养

### Page 4

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 4 
老、托育、文旅、健康等领域的部署，服务消费有望继续快于商品消费。

风险提示：外需边际走弱、地缘政治超预期恶化、AI 产业链降温超预期、
政策资金投放节奏不及预期。

### Page 7

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 7 
1. GDP：实际增长边际回升 
实际 GDP 边际回升， 名义增长继续快于实际增长。三季度经济增长呈现三个特征，
一是实际增长较二季度有改善。 三季度实际GDP 同比增长 4.5%， 高于二季度的4.3%，
但仍略低于上半年平均水平；前三季度 GDP 累计增长 4.6%，较上半年回落 0.1 个百分
点。分产业看，实际 GDP 回升主要来自第二产业改善。第二产业增加值增速由二季度
的 3.0%回升至 3.4%， 第三产业由5.1%小幅升至 5.2%， 第一产业则由3.7%回落至 3.6%。
二是名义 GDP 继续快于实际 GDP，第二产业价格修复仍是重要支撑。 根据现价计算，
三季度名义 GDP 同比增长约 6.0%，较二季度提高 0.1 个百分点，高于实际 GDP 增速
1.5 个百分点。按名义与实际增速差近似估算，三季度 GDP 平减指数约为 1.5%，较二
季度的 1.6%略有回落， 指向整体价格修复仍在延续， 但没有进一步加快。 结构上， 第二
产业价格为主要支撑。二季度，第一产业平减指数由-3.3%回升至-1.7%，第三产业则由
1.3%回落至 0.7%， 第二产业平减指数约3.0%升至 3.6%， 其中工业由3.8%上升至 4.4%，
其表现与国际能源价格上涨以及 AI 产业链、 有色和电子设备价格较强基本一致。三是内
需端对经济增长的支撑边际回落，其中投资为最大拖累，外需端支撑边际增强。 支出法
看， 前三季度最终消费支出对经济增长的贡献率为45.7%， 较上半年回落0.3 个百分点；
资本形成总额贡献率为 28.3%，较上半年回落 7.8 个百分点；货物和服务净出口贡献率
为 26.1%，较上半年提升 8.2 个百分点。 
图1 三季度实际 GDP 边际回升（%）

图2 GDP 名义增速继续高于实际增速（%）

图3 GDP 环比增速（%）

2. 生产：工业继续回升 
工业生产继续回升，采矿业和电热燃水业改善显著。9 月，规上工业增加值同比增
长 5.5%，较 8 月提高 0.3 个百分点，环比增长 0.6%。总量上看，出口对工业生产的拉
动延续。9 月，出口交货值同比增长 17.0%，较 8 月提高 5.9 个百分点。结构上看，工
业生产回升有两方面原因。一方面，制造业增加值维持韧性，高增长仍集中在装备制造
和高技术制造领域。9 月，制造业增加值增长 6.0%，较 8 月小幅回落 0.1 个百分点，但
仍高于整体工业增速。其中，高技术制造业增长 15.3%；金属制品、通用设备、专用设
备、 铁路船舶航空航天、 电气机械和计算机通信电子设备制造业分别增长 8.2%、8.5%、
12.1%、11.7%、8.1%和 16.4%。下游消费制造业总体保持正增。农副食品加工、食品
制造、 酒饮料和精制茶、 纺织、 医药和汽车制造业分别增长4.1%、7.3%、5.5%、3.2%、
4.9%和 6.7%。另一方面，拖累项增加值边际修复。一是采矿业由负转正，对工业生产
的拖累明显减轻。9 月，采矿业增加值由下降 1.4%转为增长 1.3%，其中煤炭开采和洗
选业降幅由 5.6%收窄至 3.3%，石油和天然气开采业增速由 6.2%升至 9.8%，我们理解
部分原因是地缘局势提高能源安全和保供要求，国内油气 增储上产持续推进，为采矿业
生产提供了支撑。二是电热燃水业增速回升。电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增加
值增长 5.6%，较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点。 从实物产量看， 核电、 风电和太阳能发电分别
增长 21.5%、10.6%和 19.5%。 
服务业生产明显改善，生产性服务业延续支撑，生活性服务有修复。 9 月，服务业
生产指数同比增长 4.8%，较8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 一是数字信息和生产配套型服务业
继续保持较快增长。信息传输软件和信息技术服务业、租赁和商务服务业、交通运输仓
储和邮政业生产指数分别增长 9.8%、9.5%和 5.4%。二是服务业改善广度有提升。 住宿
和餐饮业生产指数增长 5.3%，服务业商务活动指数由 49.3%升至 50.2%，重新进入扩
张区间； 电信广播电视及卫星传输、 货币金融和保险等行业商务活动指数均位于55%以
上。

3. 投资：延续回落 
投资低位企稳。9 月， 固定资产投资累计同比下降7.2%， 降幅与 8 月持平。按累计
金额反推，9 月当月投资同比约下降 7.2%，较 8 月的下降 11.0%明显收窄； 剔除房地产
后， 当月投资同比约下降3.4%， 降幅较8 月收窄约 4.4 个百分点。 季调环比增长0.07%，
是 3 月以来首次转正。

制造业投资小幅修复， 增长仍集中在少数优势领域。9 月， 制造业累计投资下降2.1%，
降幅较 1—8 月收窄 0.2 个百分点。 结构上看， 一是正增行业仍主要集中在装备和新兴制
造领域。国家统计局公布的 13 个制造业行业中，只有 5 个保持正增长，其中铁路船舶
航空航天、 计算机通信电子设备制造业投资分别增长15.8%和 9.6%， 纺织、 电气机械和
通用设备制造业分别增长 4.2%、1.2%和 1.1%。高技术产业投资增长 4.9%，其中信息
服务、 航空航天器及设备、 电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长20.2%、12.5%和 7.6%。
二是上游原材料和下游消费制造业投资仍较弱。 农副食品加工、 食品制造、 化工、 医药、
有色金属冶炼、 金属制品、 专用设备和汽车制造业投资分别下降2.5%、10.3%、7.5%、
8.1%、5.1%、10.1%、8.9%和 6.3%。

4. 消费：改善相对明显，服务强于商品 
消费是内需端改善最明显的分项， 商品消费仍受汽车拖累。9 月， 社零同比增长1.1%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.35%，剔除汽车后社零同比增长 3.9%，较 8 月
加快 1.4 个百分点。 结构上看， 商品和餐饮消费同步恢复。9 月， 商品零售额增长1.0%，
较 8 月提高 0.7 个百分点； 餐饮收入增长2.1%， 提高1.0 个百分点。 从更广义的消费口
径看， 服务消费仍是消费的重要支撑。 三季度， 社会消费商品和服务零售总额增长2.6%，
其中服务零售额增长 5.0%， 明显高于商品零售额的 1.0%； 网上服务零售额增长5.3%。
通讯信息、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务零售额保持较快增长。商品消费方面，必选消
费和通讯器材维持韧性，汽车是主要拖累。服装、化妆品、日用品、家电和中西药品零
售额分别增长 1.2%、5.4%、4.1%、1.9%和 5.0%，通讯器材类增长 31.1%。汽车零售
额下降 21.2%， 降幅较8 月扩大 2.7 个百分点； 家具、 建筑装潢材料仍分别下降3.0%和
9.2%，降幅较 8 月有所收窄。

5. 展望：有望延续生产较强、内需修复格局 
展望后续，四季度经济有望延续生产较强、需求渐进修复的格局，未来增量支撑主
要来自两条线索。总体看，三季度实际增长边际企稳，优势制造业、外需和服务消费构
成主要支撑。 往后看， 增量支撑线索有两方面。 一方面， 以 “六张网” 和8000 亿元新型
政策性金融工具为抓手，加快资金投放和项目开工。9 月以来三家政策性银行投放的新
型政策性金融工具资金已经超过 1000 亿元，建筑业 PMI 和新订单指数同步回升，指向
政策正在向项目端传导。不过，从补充资本金、落实配套融资到形成施工和实物工作量
仍存在时滞，基建对传统建筑链的拉动或更多在四季度后半段逐步显现。另一方面，服
务消费仍是扩大内需更具确定性的方向。9 月多部门出台金融支持服务业扩能提质政策，
叠加扩大消费“十五五”规划对养老、托育、文旅、健康等领域的部署，服务消费有望
继续快于商品消费。 
风险提示 
外需边际走弱、地缘政治超预期恶化、AI 产业链降温超预期、政策资金投放节奏不
及预期。

投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对同期基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对同期基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对同期基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对同期基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years. 1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.

Eurozone: Amid rising inflation, the business climate remains resilient, but consumer

confidence falls back
02/10/2026 3
We have selected two inflation measures (with and without energy) and six survey indicators: 1/ & 2/ business confidence, as
measured by the PMIs in the manufacturing and services sectors; 3/ & 4/ input and output prices components of the
composite PMI (in order to identify direct inflationary pressures); 5/ the suppliers’ delivery times component of the
manufacturing PMI (a direct indicator of possible supply difficulties and supply-demand imbalance, and therefore, indirectly,

of inflationary pressures in the making); and 6/ consumer confidence (measured by the “assessment of financial situation in
the next 12 months” in order to capture the impact of inflation on purchasing power). The trends in each of these indicators
are observed relative to month m=0, corresponding to the start of the conflict. Each line does not represent the level of the
indicator, but its cumulative variation compared to month m=0.
The overall picture from the confidence surveys available for September is globally positive. According to the PMIs, the

business climate in services has indeed seen a clear improvement, and that in the manufacturing sector remains comfortably
in expansion territory for the 8 th month in a row. However, consumer confidence has recorded its first decline in 5 months,
likely driven by rising inflation and concerns over purchasing power. News on the inflation front is negative. PMI surveys
report a strengthening of inflationary pressures: this trend is not a surprise given the tensions in energy prices, but it is

nonetheless significant. As for inflation itself, it continues to rise, more markedly in September: +0.6 percentage points, to
3.8% year-on-year (Eurostat flash estimate), of which 0.4 pp du to the higher energy contribution. Inflation excluding energy
increased as well, by 0.2 pp to 2.3% (of which 0.1 pp due to the rise in food inflation).
Source: S&P Global, Eurostat, European Commission, Macrobond, BNP Paribas

Document 303: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.
